People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi. Yeah, thanks. Um, I guess I'm here because lately I just can't keep my head clear at work. It's getting really embarrassing, honestly. Like, I'll be in a session and I completely lose track of what my clients are saying. 

Just yesterday, this one client was telling me about his garden—he grows these huge heirloom tomatoes, the purple kind? My grandma used to grow those when I was a kid, actually. We'd eat them right off the vine with a little salt in the summer, they were so good. I haven't had a good tomato in years, the ones at the store are always so watery... 

But, um... yeah, anyway, I was sitting there with him and realized I hadn't heard a single word he said for like ten minutes. And it's not just listening. When I go to do my paperwork, I end up rereading the exact same chart note over and over again. I just stare at it. I freeze up on really simple decisions I used to handle without even thinking. I don't know what's going on with me.

Um, I'd say it's been going on for about a month? Yeah, right around the time the clinic lost a bunch of staff. One of the guys who left, Dave, he actually moved out to Colorado to do that wilderness therapy stuff. Which sounds amazing, honestly. I used to love hiking. I went up to the state park last fall and the leaves were just... incredible. I bought these new boots for it too, but they gave me the worst blisters on my heels. I tried those gel pads, but they just slide around when you sweat. 

But anyway, because Dave and a couple others quit, management shifted my schedule all around, so now I'm stuck doing late-evening coverage. And with that comes way heavier crisis paperwork. The forms are just ridiculous, and the printer in the breakroom always jams if you put more than ten sheets in the tray at once. I told the office manager about it like three times, but she's always busy ordering supplies and... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, it's been about a month since all that schedule stuff changed.

Um, sad? I don't know if sad is the right word. I guess I've just been feeling really... heavy? Like, physically heavy. Like I'm walking through mud all the time. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, not really. I just... I don't care about much of anything anymore. Like, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner of my room gathering dust. It's a Fender Stratocaster, actually. I saved up for like a year to buy it when I was in high school. I used to play in this little garage band with my buddy Mark. Mark's actually the one who taught me how to change the strings. He's really good with his hands—he works as a mechanic now over at that shop on Elm Street. The one with the big neon sign that's always flickering? I keep meaning to tell him he should get that fixed, it's super distracting when you drive by at night. 

But yeah, I haven't picked up the guitar in weeks. And I keep bailing on my Sunday soccer league, too. I just text them and say I'm busy. I've been bailing on my volunteer shifts at the animal shelter, too. Which sucks because I really like the dogs. There's this one pitbull mix, Buster, he has this funny little underbite. He always tries to carry three tennis balls in his mouth at once and he just drops them everywhere. It's hilarious. Tennis balls are getting so expensive though, have you noticed that? I bought a can the other day and it was like five bucks. For three balls! It's crazy. 

But um, yeah, even when I force myself to do stuff I usually like, like cooking or going hiking, it just... it feels like I'm just going through the motions. Like there's no point to it. I just don't get anything out of it anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, weight? Yeah, I guess so. My pants have gotten pretty loose lately. I've been having to wear this belt I haven't worn since high school. It's this weird braided leather one I got at a thrift store when I was going through this phase where I only wore vintage clothes. Thrift stores used to be so much better, you know? Now they just have fast fashion stuff that falls apart after one wash. I tried to sew a button back on one of my shirts the other day, but the thread just kept snapping. I think the thread was old, it came from my grandma's sewing kit. She had this huge tin of Danish butter cookies, but it was just filled with needles and spools of thread. I think every grandma does that, right? It's like a universal disappointment when you open it hoping for a cookie. 

But um, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. Yeah, most days I don't even feel hungry until someone reminds me to eat. Like, I'll just skip meals without really meaning to. My buddy Mark came over yesterday and ordered a pizza because he realized I hadn't eaten all day. He always gets pineapple on his pizza, which I used to think was gross, but it's actually not that bad if you add jalapeños. The sweet and spicy thing kind of works. But yeah, I just... I forget to eat, I guess.

He's really good with his hands—he works as a mechanic now over at that shop on Elm Street. The one with the big neon sign that's always flickering? I keep meaning to tell him he should get that fixed, it's super distracting when you drive by at night. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't picked up the guitar in weeks. And I keep bailing on my Sunday soccer league, too. I just text them and say I'm busy. I've been bailing on my volunteer shifts at the animal shelter, too. Which sucks because I really like the dogs. There's this one pitbull mix, Buster, he has this funny little underbite. He always tries to carry three tennis balls in his mouth at once and he just drops them everywhere. It's hilarious. Tennis balls are getting so expensive though, have you noticed that? I bought a can the other day and it was like five bucks. For three balls! It's crazy. \n\nBut um, yeah, even when I force myself to do stuff I usually like, like cooking or going hiking, it just... it feels like I'm just going through the motions. Like there's no point to it. I just don't get anything out of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that normally would give you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Um, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, was the last one about enjoying things? I guess it's kind of the same thing, right? Like, losing interest and not enjoying stuff. But yeah, I don't really have any interest in much. It's like I was saying about the soccer league and the guitar. Even video games... I bought that new RPG everyone's talking about, the one with the huge open world? My buddy Mark—the mechanic—he's already logged like eighty hours on it. He plays on his PC, though. He built this crazy custom rig with all these LED lights inside the case. It looks like a spaceship or something. I don't know how he affords it, honestly, pc parts are so expensive right now. I read an article saying it's because of some shortage with the microchips they make overseas. \n\nBut um, yeah, I bought the game for my console, played it for maybe twenty minutes, and just turned it off. I didn't even care about creating my character, which is usually my favorite part. I just hit randomize and started. So yeah, I guess I have lost interest. It's just all... whatever.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, weight? Yeah, I guess so. My pants have gotten pretty loose lately. I've been having to wear this belt I haven't worn since high school. It's this weird braided leather one I got at a thrift store when I was going through this phase where I only wore vintage clothes. Thrift stores used to be so much better, you know? Now they just have fast fashion stuff that falls apart after one wash. I tried to sew a button back on one of my shirts the other day, but the thread just kept snapping. I think the thread was old, it came from my grandma's sewing kit. She had this huge tin of Danish butter cookies, but it was just filled with needles and spools of thread. I think every grandma does that, right? It's like a universal disappointment when you open it hoping for a cookie. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. Yeah, most days I don't even feel hungry until someone reminds me to eat. Like, I'll just skip meals without really meaning to. My buddy Mark came over yesterday and ordered a pizza because he realized I hadn't eaten all day. He always gets pineapple on his pizza, which I used to think was gross, but it's actually not that bad if you add jalapeños. The sweet and spicy thing kind of works. But yeah, I just... I forget to eat, I guess.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, sleeping? Yeah, it's been pretty bad actually. Like, I get home from work and I'm just so exhausted, you know? But then I get into bed and I just lie there for hours. My brain just keeps replaying everything from the day. It's so frustrating because my mattress is actually really comfortable. I got it on sale last year during one of those Presidents' Day blowouts. The guy at the store was so pushy though, he kept trying to upsell me on this adjustable base that had built-in massage motors. Who even needs that? Plus, it was like an extra thousand dollars. I just wanted a regular bed. I ended up getting a memory foam one, which is nice, but it gets kind of hot in the summer. I had to buy these special cooling sheets online. They're made of bamboo, supposedly? I don't know how they turn bamboo into fabric, it seems like it would be really scratchy, but they're actually super soft. 

But anyway, yeah, I just lie there in the dark. And then if I do finally manage to fall asleep, I always end up waking up way before my alarm goes off. And I just feel completely drained, like I barely slept at all. It's just... I'm always tired.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, guilty? Yeah, pretty much constantly. Like at work... I work at this clinic, and if I miss even one little detail in a note or forget to return a call right away, I just completely spiral. I keep telling myself I'm useless and that I'm letting the clients down, and everyone else at the clinic too. 

We have to do so much paperwork, and they make us use these specific blue pens for the physical files. The office manager is totally obsessed with them, she orders them in bulk. They're those gel ones, you know? The ink is super smooth but if you don't let it dry for like ten seconds it smears everywhere. I ruined a whole page once because I dragged my sleeve across it. I was wearing this sweater my aunt knitted for me. She's really into knitting right now, she made me this scarf for Christmas but it's ridiculously long. Like, it literally drags on the floor if I don't wrap it around my neck at least three times. She lives up in Minnesota so I guess she needs really thick winter stuff, but it's just impractical for me. Plus the yarn she uses sheds everywhere, I'm always picking little fuzzies off my car seats. I drive an older Civic and the upholstery is that weird velvet material that just grabs onto everything. 

But um... wait, what was the question? Oh, blaming myself. Yeah. If I mess up a note or am late on a phone call, I just feel like I'm a complete failure to everyone there. It really weighs on me.

You know the one? They've had it down to one lane for like three months now. The guy holding the stop sign always looks so bored. I feel bad for him, standing out there in the heat. It was so hot yesterday, I actually had to turn the AC on in my apartment for the first time this year. My AC unit makes this terrible rattling noise, though. I think a screw is loose or something. I tried to fix it myself with this little toolkit I got for Christmas a few years ago, but none of the screwdrivers fit. It's one of those cheap kits, you know? The kind that comes in a flimsy plastic case. I really need to get a proper set of tools. But anyway... sad? I don't know. Just... drained, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before these concentration problems started?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I just... I don't care about much of anything anymore. Like, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner of my room gathering dust. It's a Fender Stratocaster, actually. I saved up for like a year to buy it when I was in high school. I used to play in this little garage band with my buddy Mark. Mark's actually the one who taught me how to change the strings. He's really good with his hands—he works as a mechanic now over at that shop on Elm Street. The one with the big neon sign that's always flickering? I keep meaning to tell him he should get that fixed, it's super distracting when you drive by at night. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't picked up the guitar in weeks. And I keep bailing on my Sunday soccer league, too. I just text them and say I'm busy. I've been bailing on my volunteer shifts at the animal shelter, too. Which sucks because I really like the dogs. There's this one pitbull mix, Buster, he has this funny little underbite. He always tries to carry three tennis balls in his mouth at once and he just drops them everywhere. It's hilarious. Tennis balls are getting so expensive though, have you noticed that? I bought a can the other day and it was like five bucks. For three balls! It's crazy. \n\nBut um, yeah, even when I force myself to do stuff I usually like, like cooking or going hiking, it just... it feels like I'm just going through the motions. Like there's no point to it. I just don't get anything out of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that normally would give you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Um, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, was the last one about enjoying things? I guess it's kind of the same thing, right? Like, losing interest and not enjoying stuff. But yeah, I don't really have any interest in much. It's like I was saying about the soccer league and the guitar. Even video games... I bought that new RPG everyone's talking about, the one with the huge open world? My buddy Mark—the mechanic—he's already logged like eighty hours on it. He plays on his PC, though. He built this crazy custom rig with all these LED lights inside the case. It looks like a spaceship or something. I don't know how he affords it, honestly, pc parts are so expensive right now. I read an article saying it's because of some shortage with the microchips they make overseas. \n\nBut um, yeah, I bought the game for my console, played it for maybe twenty minutes, and just turned it off. I didn't even care about creating my character, which is usually my favorite part. I just hit randomize and started. So yeah, I guess I have lost interest. It's just all... whatever.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, weight? Yeah, I guess so. My pants have gotten pretty loose lately. I've been having to wear this belt I haven't worn since high school. It's this weird braided leather one I got at a thrift store when I was going through this phase where I only wore vintage clothes. Thrift stores used to be so much better, you know? Now they just have fast fashion stuff that falls apart after one wash. I tried to sew a button back on one of my shirts the other day, but the thread just kept snapping. I think the thread was old, it came from my grandma's sewing kit. She had this huge tin of Danish butter cookies, but it was just filled with needles and spools of thread. I think every grandma does that, right? It's like a universal disappointment when you open it hoping for a cookie. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. Yeah, most days I don't even feel hungry until someone reminds me to eat. Like, I'll just skip meals without really meaning to. My buddy Mark came over yesterday and ordered a pizza because he realized I hadn't eaten all day. He always gets pineapple on his pizza, which I used to think was gross, but it's actually not that bad if you add jalapeños. The sweet and spicy thing kind of works. But yeah, I just... I forget to eat, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Um, sleeping? Yeah, it's been pretty bad actually. Like, I get home from work and I'm just so exhausted, you know? But then I get into bed and I just lie there for hours. My brain just keeps replaying everything from the day. It's so frustrating because my mattress is actually really comfortable. I got it on sale last year during one of those Presidents' Day blowouts. The guy at the store was so pushy though, he kept trying to upsell me on this adjustable base that had built-in massage motors. Who even needs that? Plus, it was like an extra thousand dollars. I just wanted a regular bed. I ended up getting a memory foam one, which is nice, but it gets kind of hot in the summer. I had to buy these special cooling sheets online. They're made of bamboo, supposedly? I don't know how they turn bamboo into fabric, it seems like it would be really scratchy, but they're actually super soft. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just lie there in the dark. And then if I do finally manage to fall asleep, I always end up waking up way before my alarm goes off. And I just feel completely drained, like I barely slept at all. It's just... I'm always tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty? Yeah, pretty much constantly. Like at work... I work at this clinic, and if I miss even one little detail in a note or forget to return a call right away, I just completely spiral. I keep telling myself I'm useless and that I'm letting the clients down, and everyone else at the clinic too. \n\nWe have to do so much paperwork, and they make us use these specific blue pens for the physical files. The office manager is totally obsessed with them, she orders them in bulk. They're those gel ones, you know? The ink is super smooth but if you don't let it dry for like ten seconds it smears everywhere. I ruined a whole page once because I dragged my sleeve across it. I was wearing this sweater my aunt knitted for me. She's really into knitting right now, she made me this scarf for Christmas but it's ridiculously long. Like, it literally drags on the floor if I don't wrap it around my neck at least three times. She lives up in Minnesota so I guess she needs really thick winter stuff, but it's just impractical for me. Plus the yarn she uses sheds everywhere, I'm always picking little fuzzies off my car seats. I drive an older Civic and the upholstery is that weird velvet material that just grabs onto everything. \n\nBut um... wait, what was the question? Oh, blaming myself. Yeah. If I mess up a note or am late on a phone call, I just feel like I'm a complete failure to everyone there. It really weighs on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, slower? No, I don't really think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I still walk at my normal pace, I guess. I actually had to run to catch the bus the other day because my car was in the shop. The mechanic said it was the alternator, which is so annoying because I just got the battery replaced like two months ago. Cars are just complete money pits, honestly. \n\nAnd the bus system here is so weird anyway. I was sitting next to this guy who was listening to a podcast with no headphones. Just blasting it right out of his phone speaker on the bus. Who even does that? And the worst part was he was listening to it on like, double speed. Everyone sounded like chipmunks. I don't get how people can process information that fast. I like to take my time when I listen to things. I used to listen to audiobooks on my commute, mostly sci-fi stuff. There was this one about terraforming Mars that was like forty hours long. The narrator had this really deep, soothing voice, kind of like that guy from those nature documentaries. I love documentaries about the ocean, even though the deep sea is absolutely terrifying to me. Like, those anglerfish with the glowing things on their heads? No thank you. Nature is just crazy sometimes. \n\nBut, um... wait, sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, moving slower. Right. No, I don't think so. I feel like I'm moving around and talking pretty much the same as I always do.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, hurting myself? No, no, I don't... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But, um, I do keep having these thoughts, like... like it would just be easier if I went to sleep and didn't wake up. And that actually really scares me, you know? Because working at the clinic, I know exactly what those kinds of thoughts can mean. 

We actually had a training on crisis protocols last month. The presenter was this guy who kept pacing back and forth across the front of the room the entire time. It was so distracting. He was wearing these really squeaky shoes, like every time he turned around on the linoleum it made this awful high-pitched noise. It reminded me of when I played basketball in high school. Our gym floor was always super dusty so we'd have to wipe the bottoms of our shoes with our hands to get any traction. I wasn't very good, honestly, I mostly just sat on the bench. Our coach was this intense guy who used to yell until his face turned purple. He made us run suicides until people were literally throwing up in the trash cans. I don't miss high school at all. The cafeteria food was always so terrible, especially on Thursdays when they served this mystery meatloaf that bounced if you dropped it. I usually just brought a sandwich, but my mom would always pack it with that cheap yellow mustard instead of the spicy brown kind I liked. 

But um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the thoughts. Yeah, I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but just... thinking about not waking up. It's scary.

Um, pleasure? Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to do a lot of stuff, but now... I don't know, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner of my room gathering dust. I haven't picked it up in weeks. It's an acoustic one, a Taylor. I saved up for like a year to buy it when I was working at this coffee shop downtown. 

That place was always so chaotic, especially during the morning rush. The espresso machine we had was constantly breaking down. It would make this horrible hissing sound and then just shoot steam everywhere. My manager refused to buy a new one because she said it added "character" to the shop. She was really into vintage stuff, always wearing these giant round glasses and floral dresses from the seventies. She actually made us use these mismatched antique mugs for the dine-in orders, which was a nightmare to stack in the dishwasher. We had to load them so carefully or the handles would snap off. I cut my finger pretty bad on a broken one once and had to go to urgent care. The waiting room there had this huge fish tank taking up a whole wall, but it only had like three tiny fish in it. It looked so empty and sad, just these little neon tetras swimming around in circles. I always wondered who was in charge of feeding them. Probably the receptionist, she looked completely miserable behind that glass partition. 

But um, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just... I keep bailing on my indoor soccer league, and I haven't been volunteering at the animal shelter either. Even when I try to force myself to do things I usually like, like cooking a nice meal or going for a hike, it just feels like I'm going through the motions. There's just no point to it anymore. It all just feels completely flat.

I've been having to wear this belt I haven't worn since high school. It's this weird braided leather one I got at a thrift store when I was going through this phase where I only wore vintage clothes. Thrift stores used to be so much better, you know? Now they just have fast fashion stuff that falls apart after one wash. I tried to sew a button back on one of my shirts the other day, but the thread just kept snapping. I think the thread was old, it came from my grandma's sewing kit. She had this huge tin of Danish butter cookies, but it was just filled with needles and spools of thread. I think every grandma does that, right? It's like a universal disappointment when you open it hoping for a cookie. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. Yeah, most days I don't even feel hungry until someone reminds me to eat. Like, I'll just skip meals without really meaning to. My buddy Mark came over yesterday and ordered a pizza because he realized I hadn't eaten all day. He always gets pineapple on his pizza, which I used to think was gross, but it's actually not that bad if you add jalapeños. The sweet and spicy thing kind of works. But yeah, I just... I forget to eat, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Um, sleeping? Yeah, it's been pretty bad actually. Like, I get home from work and I'm just so exhausted, you know? But then I get into bed and I just lie there for hours. My brain just keeps replaying everything from the day. It's so frustrating because my mattress is actually really comfortable. I got it on sale last year during one of those Presidents' Day blowouts. The guy at the store was so pushy though, he kept trying to upsell me on this adjustable base that had built-in massage motors. Who even needs that? Plus, it was like an extra thousand dollars. I keep telling myself I'm useless and that I'm letting the clients down, and everyone else at the clinic too. \n\nWe have to do so much paperwork, and they make us use these specific blue pens for the physical files. The office manager is totally obsessed with them, she orders them in bulk. They're those gel ones, you know? The ink is super smooth but if you don't let it dry for like ten seconds it smears everywhere. I ruined a whole page once because I dragged my sleeve across it. I was wearing this sweater my aunt knitted for me. She's really into knitting right now, she made me this scarf for Christmas but it's ridiculously long. Like, it literally drags on the floor if I don't wrap it around my neck at least three times. She lives up in Minnesota so I guess she needs really thick winter stuff, but it's just impractical for me. Plus the yarn she uses sheds everywhere, I'm always picking little fuzzies off my car seats. I drive an older Civic and the upholstery is that weird velvet material that just grabs onto everything. \n\nBut um... wait, what was the question? Oh, blaming myself. Yeah. If I mess up a note or am late on a phone call, I just feel like I'm a complete failure to everyone there. The mechanic said it was the alternator, which is so annoying because I just got the battery replaced like two months ago. Um, my thoughts? Yeah, actually. That's... that's kind of the main reason I'm here. Lately, I just can't seem to keep my head clear at work. Like, I'll be sitting in a session with a client, and I just completely lose track of what they're saying. We use this really clunky electronic health record system at the clinic. It's so outdated, it looks like it was built in the nineties. The font is tiny and you have to click through like five different screens just to schedule a follow-up appointment. My coworker, Sarah, she's always complaining about it. She has this giant ergonomic keyboard that splits down the middle because she gets carpal tunnel. It looks like a spaceship control panel on her desk. She's really into all that ergonomic stuff, she even has one of those chairs that you kneel on instead of sit. I tried it once and I felt like I was going to slide right off onto the floor. The floors in our office are this weird carpet that always has static electricity. Every time I touch the doorknob to the breakroom I get shocked. It's so annoying, especially when I'm just trying to get a cup of coffee. The coffee in the breakroom is terrible anyway, it's that cheap powdered stuff that tastes like burnt cardboard. I usually just bring my own from home in a thermos. 

But um... wait, what was I talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts being slow. Yeah, it's just... I freeze up on these really simple decisions that I used to handle with no problem. It's like my brain is just moving through molasses or something.

The espresso machine we had was constantly breaking down. It would make this horrible hissing sound and then just shoot steam everywhere. My manager refused to buy a new one because she said it added \"character\" to the shop. She was really into vintage stuff, always wearing these giant round glasses and floral dresses from the seventies. She actually made us use these mismatched antique mugs for the dine-in orders, which was a nightmare to stack in the dishwasher. We had to load them so carefully or the handles would snap off. I cut my finger pretty bad on a broken one once and had to go to urgent care. The waiting room there had this huge fish tank taking up a whole wall, but it only had like three tiny fish in it. It looked so empty and sad, just these little neon tetras swimming around in circles. I always wondered who was in charge of feeding them. Probably the receptionist, she looked completely miserable behind that glass partition. \n\nBut um, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just... I keep bailing on my indoor soccer league, and I haven't been volunteering at the animal shelter either. Even when I try to force myself to do things I usually like, like cooking a nice meal or going for a hike, it just feels like I'm going through the motions. There's just no point to it anymore. It all just feels completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Um, my thoughts? Yeah, actually. That's... that's kind of the main reason I'm here. Lately, I just can't seem to keep my head clear at work. Like, I'll be sitting in a session with a client, and I just completely lose track of what they're saying. And then I have to go back and reread the same chart note over and over again because it just won't stick in my brain. \n\nWe use this really clunky electronic health record system at the clinic. It's so outdated, it looks like it was built in the nineties. The font is tiny and you have to click through like five different screens just to schedule a follow-up appointment. My coworker, Sarah, she's always complaining about it. She has this giant ergonomic keyboard that splits down the middle because she gets carpal tunnel. It looks like a spaceship control panel on her desk. She's really into all that ergonomic stuff, she even has one of those chairs that you kneel on instead of sit. I tried it once and I felt like I was going to slide right off onto the floor. The floors in our office are this weird carpet that always has static electricity. Every time I touch the doorknob to the breakroom I get shocked. It's so annoying, especially when I'm just trying to get a cup of coffee. The coffee in the breakroom is terrible anyway, it's that cheap powdered stuff that tastes like burnt cardboard. I usually just bring my own from home in a thermos. \n\nBut um... wait, what was I talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts being slow. Yeah, it's just... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, getting better or worse? I'd say... well, it's been going on for about a month now. It all started when the clinic lost a bunch of staff, and my schedule got completely upended. I got shifted into doing all this late-evening coverage and handling way heavier crisis paperwork. 

The paperwork is just endless. We have to fill out these specific blue forms for any crisis intervention, and they're always running out of them in the copy room. The copy machine is a nightmare, honestly. It jams if you even look at it wrong. The repair guy, Dave, is there at least twice a week. He always smells faintly of peppermint and uses these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a watchmaker's kit. He told me once he builds model airplanes in his garage on the weekends. Like, the really intricate ones made of balsa wood that take months to glue together. I tried building a model car when I was a kid, a '67 Mustang, but I got glue all over the windshield and ruined it. I was so mad I threw it in the trash. My dad tried to fish it out and fix it, but the plastic was all cloudy. He used to keep his own car, this old Honda, so clean you could eat off the dashboard. He'd spend every Sunday morning washing it in the driveway with this special sponge he bought from an infomercial. 

But, uh, sorry, what was the question? Oh, right, my symptoms. Yeah, it's been about a month since the schedule change and all that extra crisis paperwork. I wouldn't say it's getting better, it's just... been steadily like this since then.

And I keep bailing on my Sunday soccer league, too. I just text them and say I'm busy. I've been bailing on my volunteer shifts at the animal shelter, too. Which sucks because I really like the dogs. There's this one pitbull mix, Buster, he has this funny little underbite. He always tries to carry three tennis balls in his mouth at once and he just drops them everywhere. It's hilarious. Tennis balls are getting so expensive though, have you noticed that? I bought a can the other day and it was like five bucks. For three balls! It's crazy. \n\nBut um, yeah, even when I force myself to do stuff I usually like, like cooking or going hiking, it just... it feels like I'm just going through the motions. Like there's no point to it. I just don't get anything out of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that normally would give you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Um, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, was the last one about enjoying things? I guess it's kind of the same thing, right? Like, losing interest and not enjoying stuff. But yeah, I don't really have any interest in much. It's like I was saying about the soccer league and the guitar. Even video games... I bought that new RPG everyone's talking about, the one with the huge open world? My buddy Mark—the mechanic—he's already logged like eighty hours on it. He plays on his PC, though. He built this crazy custom rig with all these LED lights inside the case. It looks like a spaceship or something. I don't know how he affords it, honestly, pc parts are so expensive right now. I read an article saying it's because of some shortage with the microchips they make overseas. \n\nBut um, yeah, I bought the game for my console, played it for maybe twenty minutes, and just turned it off. I didn't even care about creating my character, which is usually my favorite part. I just hit randomize and started. So yeah, I guess I have lost interest. It's just all... whatever.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, weight? Yeah, I guess so. My pants have gotten pretty loose lately. I've been having to wear this belt I haven't worn since high school. It's this weird braided leather one I got at a thrift store when I was going through this phase where I only wore vintage clothes. Thrift stores used to be so much better, you know? Now they just have fast fashion stuff that falls apart after one wash. I tried to sew a button back on one of my shirts the other day, but the thread just kept snapping. I think the thread was old, it came from my grandma's sewing kit. She had this huge tin of Danish butter cookies, but it was just filled with needles and spools of thread. I think every grandma does that, right? It's like a universal disappointment when you open it hoping for a cookie. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. Yeah, most days I don't even feel hungry until someone reminds me to eat. Like, I'll just skip meals without really meaning to. My buddy Mark came over yesterday and ordered a pizza because he realized I hadn't eaten all day. He always gets pineapple on his pizza, which I used to think was gross, but it's actually not that bad if you add jalapeños. The sweet and spicy thing kind of works. But yeah, I just... I forget to eat, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Um, sleeping? Yeah, it's been pretty bad actually. Like, I get home from work and I'm just so exhausted, you know? But then I get into bed and I just lie there for hours. My brain just keeps replaying everything from the day. It's so frustrating because my mattress is actually really comfortable. I got it on sale last year during one of those Presidents' Day blowouts. The guy at the store was so pushy though, he kept trying to upsell me on this adjustable base that had built-in massage motors. Who even needs that? Plus, it was like an extra thousand dollars. I just wanted a regular bed. I ended up getting a memory foam one, which is nice, but it gets kind of hot in the summer. I had to buy these special cooling sheets online. They're made of bamboo, supposedly? I don't know how they turn bamboo into fabric, it seems like it would be really scratchy, but they're actually super soft. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just lie there in the dark. And then if I do finally manage to fall asleep, I always end up waking up way before my alarm goes off. And I just feel completely drained, like I barely slept at all. It's just... I'm always tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty? Yeah, pretty much constantly. Like at work... I work at this clinic, and if I miss even one little detail in a note or forget to return a call right away, I just completely spiral. I keep telling myself I'm useless and that I'm letting the clients down, and everyone else at the clinic too. \n\nWe have to do so much paperwork, and they make us use these specific blue pens for the physical files. The office manager is totally obsessed with them, she orders them in bulk. They're those gel ones, you know? The ink is super smooth but if you don't let it dry for like ten seconds it smears everywhere. I ruined a whole page once because I dragged my sleeve across it. I was wearing this sweater my aunt knitted for me. She's really into knitting right now, she made me this scarf for Christmas but it's ridiculously long. Like, it literally drags on the floor if I don't wrap it around my neck at least three times. She lives up in Minnesota so I guess she needs really thick winter stuff, but it's just impractical for me. Plus the yarn she uses sheds everywhere, I'm always picking little fuzzies off my car seats. I drive an older Civic and the upholstery is that weird velvet material that just grabs onto everything. \n\nBut um... wait, what was the question? Oh, blaming myself. Yeah. If I mess up a note or am late on a phone call, I just feel like I'm a complete failure to everyone there. It really weighs on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, slower? No, I don't really think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I still walk at my normal pace, I guess. I actually had to run to catch the bus the other day because my car was in the shop. The mechanic said it was the alternator, which is so annoying because I just got the battery replaced like two months ago. Cars are just complete money pits, honestly. \n\nAnd the bus system here is so weird anyway. I was sitting next to this guy who was listening to a podcast with no headphones. Just blasting it right out of his phone speaker on the bus. Who even does that? And the worst part was he was listening to it on like, double speed. Everyone sounded like chipmunks. I don't get how people can process information that fast. I like to take my time when I listen to things. I used to listen to audiobooks on my commute, mostly sci-fi stuff. There was this one about terraforming Mars that was like forty hours long. The narrator had this really deep, soothing voice, kind of like that guy from those nature documentaries. I love documentaries about the ocean, even though the deep sea is absolutely terrifying to me. Like, those anglerfish with the glowing things on their heads? No thank you. Nature is just crazy sometimes. \n\nBut, um... wait, sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, moving slower. Right. No, I don't think so. I feel like I'm moving around and talking pretty much the same as I always do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, hurting myself? No, no, I don't... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But, um, I do keep having these thoughts, like... like it would just be easier if I went to sleep and didn't wake up. And that actually really scares me, you know? Because working at the clinic, I know exactly what those kinds of thoughts can mean. \n\nWe actually had a training on crisis protocols last month. The presenter was this guy who kept pacing back and forth across the front of the room the entire time. It was so distracting. He was wearing these really squeaky shoes, like every time he turned around on the linoleum it made this awful high-pitched noise. It reminded me of when I played basketball in high school. Our gym floor was always super dusty so we'd have to wipe the bottoms of our shoes with our hands to get any traction. I wasn't very good, honestly, I mostly just sat on the bench. Our coach was this intense guy who used to yell until his face turned purple. He made us run suicides until people were literally throwing up in the trash cans. I don't miss high school at all. The cafeteria food was always so terrible, especially on Thursdays when they served this mystery meatloaf that bounced if you dropped it. I usually just brought a sandwich, but my mom would always pack it with that cheap yellow mustard instead of the spicy brown kind I liked. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the thoughts. Yeah, I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but just... thinking about not waking up. It's scary.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, pleasure? Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to do a lot of stuff, but now... I don't know, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner of my room gathering dust. I haven't picked it up in weeks. It's an acoustic one, a Taylor. I saved up for like a year to buy it when I was working at this coffee shop downtown. \n\nThat place was always so chaotic, especially during the morning rush. The espresso machine we had was constantly breaking down. It would make this horrible hissing sound and then just shoot steam everywhere. My manager refused to buy a new one because she said it added \"character\" to the shop. She was really into vintage stuff, always wearing these giant round glasses and floral dresses from the seventies. She actually made us use these mismatched antique mugs for the dine-in orders, which was a nightmare to stack in the dishwasher. We had to load them so carefully or the handles would snap off. I cut my finger pretty bad on a broken one once and had to go to urgent care. The waiting room there had this huge fish tank taking up a whole wall, but it only had like three tiny fish in it. It looked so empty and sad, just these little neon tetras swimming around in circles. I always wondered who was in charge of feeding them. Probably the receptionist, she looked completely miserable behind that glass partition. \n\nBut um, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just... I keep bailing on my indoor soccer league, and I haven't been volunteering at the animal shelter either. Even when I try to force myself to do things I usually like, like cooking a nice meal or going for a hike, it just feels like I'm going through the motions. There's just no point to it anymore. It all just feels completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Um, my thoughts? Yeah, actually. That's... that's kind of the main reason I'm here. Lately, I just can't seem to keep my head clear at work. Like, I'll be sitting in a session with a client, and I just completely lose track of what they're saying. And then I have to go back and reread the same chart note over and over again because it just won't stick in my brain. \n\nWe use this really clunky electronic health record system at the clinic. It's so outdated, it looks like it was built in the nineties. The font is tiny and you have to click through like five different screens just to schedule a follow-up appointment. My coworker, Sarah, she's always complaining about it. She has this giant ergonomic keyboard that splits down the middle because she gets carpal tunnel. It looks like a spaceship control panel on her desk. She's really into all that ergonomic stuff, she even has one of those chairs that you kneel on instead of sit. I tried it once and I felt like I was going to slide right off onto the floor. The floors in our office are this weird carpet that always has static electricity. Every time I touch the doorknob to the breakroom I get shocked. It's so annoying, especially when I'm just trying to get a cup of coffee. The coffee in the breakroom is terrible anyway, it's that cheap powdered stuff that tastes like burnt cardboard. I usually just bring my own from home in a thermos. \n\nBut um... wait, what was I talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts being slow. Yeah, it's just... I freeze up on these really simple decisions that I used to handle with no problem. It's like my brain is just moving through molasses or something.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting better, worse, or staying about the same over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um, getting better or worse? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, restless? No, I don't really think so. I haven't noticed anything like that. I mostly just sit at my desk all day anyway. 

My desk is right next to this huge window, which sounds nice, but the sun hits it right around 3 PM and totally blinds me. I had to tape up this piece of cardboard just to see my monitor. The maintenance guy said he'd install blinds, but that was like three weeks ago. He's always busy fixing the elevator. That thing is terrifying, honestly. It shudders every time it stops on the second floor, and the buttons are all worn down so you can't even read the numbers anymore. Last week, it got stuck for like ten minutes while I was in there with the FedEx guy. He was super chill about it, though. He just kept listening to his podcast on his AirPods. It was some true crime thing about a bank robbery in the eighties. I used to listen to true crime podcasts, but they started giving me weird dreams. Like, I dreamt I was trying to hide a duffel bag full of money in my mom's basement, but the basement was completely flooded with jello. Cherry jello, specifically. It was so bizarre. 

But um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, moving around. No, I don't really feel restless. I just feel kind of stuck, I guess.

I tried building a model car when I was a kid, a '67 Mustang, but I got glue all over the windshield and ruined it. I was so mad I threw it in the trash. My dad tried to fish it out and fix it, but the plastic was all cloudy. He used to keep his own car, this old Honda, so clean you could eat off the dashboard. I've been having to wear this belt I haven't worn since high school. It's this weird braided leather one I got at a thrift store when I was going through this phase where I only wore vintage clothes. Thrift stores used to be so much better, you know? Now they just have fast fashion stuff that falls apart after one wash. I tried to sew a button back on one of my shirts the other day, but the thread just kept snapping. I think the thread was old, it came from my grandma's sewing kit. She had this huge tin of Danish butter cookies, but it was just filled with needles and spools of thread. I think every grandma does that, right? It's like a universal disappointment when you open it hoping for a cookie. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. Yeah, most days I don't even feel hungry until someone reminds me to eat. Like, I'll just skip meals without really meaning to. My buddy Mark came over yesterday and ordered a pizza because he realized I hadn't eaten all day. He always gets pineapple on his pizza, which I used to think was gross, but it's actually not that bad if you add jalapeños. The sweet and spicy thing kind of works. But yeah, I just... I forget to eat, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Um, sleeping? Yeah, it's been pretty bad actually. Like, I get home from work and I'm just so exhausted, you know? But then I get into bed and I just lie there for hours. My brain just keeps replaying everything from the day. It's so frustrating because my mattress is actually really comfortable. I got it on sale last year during one of those Presidents' Day blowouts. The guy at the store was so pushy though, he kept trying to upsell me on this adjustable base that had built-in massage motors. Who even needs that? Plus, it was like an extra thousand dollars. I just wanted a regular bed. I ended up getting a memory foam one, which is nice, but it gets kind of hot in the summer. I had to buy these special cooling sheets online. They're made of bamboo, supposedly? I don't know how they turn bamboo into fabric, it seems like it would be really scratchy, but they're actually super soft. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just lie there in the dark. And then if I do finally manage to fall asleep, I always end up waking up way before my alarm goes off. And I just feel completely drained, like I barely slept at all. It's just... I'm always tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty? Yeah, pretty much constantly. Like at work... I work at this clinic, and if I miss even one little detail in a note or forget to return a call right away, I just completely spiral. I keep telling myself I'm useless and that I'm letting the clients down, and everyone else at the clinic too. \n\nWe have to do so much paperwork, and they make us use these specific blue pens for the physical files. The office manager is totally obsessed with them, she orders them in bulk. They're those gel ones, you know? The ink is super smooth but if you don't let it dry for like ten seconds it smears everywhere. I ruined a whole page once because I dragged my sleeve across it. I was wearing this sweater my aunt knitted for me. She's really into knitting right now, she made me this scarf for Christmas but it's ridiculously long. Like, it literally drags on the floor if I don't wrap it around my neck at least three times. She lives up in Minnesota so I guess she needs really thick winter stuff, but it's just impractical for me. Plus the yarn she uses sheds everywhere, I'm always picking little fuzzies off my car seats. I drive an older Civic and the upholstery is that weird velvet material that just grabs onto everything. \n\nBut um... wait, what was the question? Oh, blaming myself. Yeah. If I mess up a note or am late on a phone call, I just feel like I'm a complete failure to everyone there. It really weighs on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, slower? No, I don't really think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I still walk at my normal pace, I guess. I actually had to run to catch the bus the other day because my car was in the shop. The mechanic said it was the alternator, which is so annoying because I just got the battery replaced like two months ago. Cars are just complete money pits, honestly. \n\nAnd the bus system here is so weird anyway. I was sitting next to this guy who was listening to a podcast with no headphones. Just blasting it right out of his phone speaker on the bus. Who even does that? And the worst part was he was listening to it on like, double speed. Everyone sounded like chipmunks. I don't get how people can process information that fast. I like to take my time when I listen to things. I used to listen to audiobooks on my commute, mostly sci-fi stuff. There was this one about terraforming Mars that was like forty hours long. The narrator had this really deep, soothing voice, kind of like that guy from those nature documentaries. I love documentaries about the ocean, even though the deep sea is absolutely terrifying to me. Like, those anglerfish with the glowing things on their heads? No thank you. Nature is just crazy sometimes. \n\nBut, um... wait, sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, moving slower. Right. No, I don't think so. I feel like I'm moving around and talking pretty much the same as I always do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, hurting myself? No, no, I don't... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But, um, I do keep having these thoughts, like... like it would just be easier if I went to sleep and didn't wake up. And that actually really scares me, you know? Because working at the clinic, I know exactly what those kinds of thoughts can mean. \n\nWe actually had a training on crisis protocols last month. The presenter was this guy who kept pacing back and forth across the front of the room the entire time. It was so distracting. He was wearing these really squeaky shoes, like every time he turned around on the linoleum it made this awful high-pitched noise. It reminded me of when I played basketball in high school. Our gym floor was always super dusty so we'd have to wipe the bottoms of our shoes with our hands to get any traction. I wasn't very good, honestly, I mostly just sat on the bench. Our coach was this intense guy who used to yell until his face turned purple. He made us run suicides until people were literally throwing up in the trash cans. I don't miss high school at all. The cafeteria food was always so terrible, especially on Thursdays when they served this mystery meatloaf that bounced if you dropped it. I usually just brought a sandwich, but my mom would always pack it with that cheap yellow mustard instead of the spicy brown kind I liked. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the thoughts. Yeah, I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but just... thinking about not waking up. It's scary.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, pleasure? Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to do a lot of stuff, but now... I don't know, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner of my room gathering dust. I haven't picked it up in weeks. It's an acoustic one, a Taylor. I saved up for like a year to buy it when I was working at this coffee shop downtown. \n\nThat place was always so chaotic, especially during the morning rush. The espresso machine we had was constantly breaking down. It would make this horrible hissing sound and then just shoot steam everywhere. My manager refused to buy a new one because she said it added \"character\" to the shop. She was really into vintage stuff, always wearing these giant round glasses and floral dresses from the seventies. She actually made us use these mismatched antique mugs for the dine-in orders, which was a nightmare to stack in the dishwasher. We had to load them so carefully or the handles would snap off. I cut my finger pretty bad on a broken one once and had to go to urgent care. The waiting room there had this huge fish tank taking up a whole wall, but it only had like three tiny fish in it. It looked so empty and sad, just these little neon tetras swimming around in circles. I always wondered who was in charge of feeding them. Probably the receptionist, she looked completely miserable behind that glass partition. \n\nBut um, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just... I keep bailing on my indoor soccer league, and I haven't been volunteering at the animal shelter either. Even when I try to force myself to do things I usually like, like cooking a nice meal or going for a hike, it just feels like I'm going through the motions. There's just no point to it anymore. It all just feels completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Um, my thoughts? Yeah, actually. That's... that's kind of the main reason I'm here. Lately, I just can't seem to keep my head clear at work. Like, I'll be sitting in a session with a client, and I just completely lose track of what they're saying. And then I have to go back and reread the same chart note over and over again because it just won't stick in my brain. \n\nWe use this really clunky electronic health record system at the clinic. It's so outdated, it looks like it was built in the nineties. The font is tiny and you have to click through like five different screens just to schedule a follow-up appointment. My coworker, Sarah, she's always complaining about it. She has this giant ergonomic keyboard that splits down the middle because she gets carpal tunnel. It looks like a spaceship control panel on her desk. She's really into all that ergonomic stuff, she even has one of those chairs that you kneel on instead of sit. I tried it once and I felt like I was going to slide right off onto the floor. The floors in our office are this weird carpet that always has static electricity. Every time I touch the doorknob to the breakroom I get shocked. It's so annoying, especially when I'm just trying to get a cup of coffee. The coffee in the breakroom is terrible anyway, it's that cheap powdered stuff that tastes like burnt cardboard. I usually just bring my own from home in a thermos. \n\nBut um... wait, what was I talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts being slow. Yeah, it's just... I freeze up on these really simple decisions that I used to handle with no problem. It's like my brain is just moving through molasses or something.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting better, worse, or staying about the same over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um, getting better or worse? I'd say... well, it's been going on for about a month now. It all started when the clinic lost a bunch of staff, and my schedule got completely upended. I got shifted into doing all this late-evening coverage and handling way heavier crisis paperwork. \n\nThe paperwork is just endless. We have to fill out these specific blue forms for any crisis intervention, and they're always running out of them in the copy room. The copy machine is a nightmare, honestly. It jams if you even look at it wrong. The repair guy, Dave, is there at least twice a week. He always smells faintly of peppermint and uses these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a watchmaker's kit. He told me once he builds model airplanes in his garage on the weekends. Like, the really intricate ones made of balsa wood that take months to glue together. I tried building a model car when I was a kid, a '67 Mustang, but I got glue all over the windshield and ruined it. I was so mad I threw it in the trash. My dad tried to fish it out and fix it, but the plastic was all cloudy. He used to keep his own car, this old Honda, so clean you could eat off the dashboard. He'd spend every Sunday morning washing it in the driveway with this special sponge he bought from an infomercial. \n\nBut, uh, sorry, what was the question? Oh, right, my symptoms. Yeah, it's been about a month since the schedule change and all that extra crisis paperwork. I wouldn't say it's getting better, it's just... been steadily like this since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving or acting more restlessly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, restless? I mostly just sit at my desk all day anyway. \n\nMy desk is right next to this huge window, which sounds nice, but the sun hits it right around 3 PM and totally blinds me. I had to tape up this piece of cardboard just to see my monitor. The maintenance guy said he'd install blinds, but that was like three weeks ago. He's always busy fixing the elevator. That thing is terrifying, honestly. It shudders every time it stops on the second floor, and the buttons are all worn down so you can't even read the numbers anymore. Last week, it got stuck for like ten minutes while I was in there with the FedEx guy. He was super chill about it, though. He just kept listening to his podcast on his AirPods. It was some true crime thing about a bank robbery in the eighties. I used to listen to true crime podcasts, but they started giving me weird dreams. Like, I dreamt I was trying to hide a duffel bag full of money in my mom's basement, but the basement was completely flooded with jello. Cherry jello, specifically. It was so bizarre. \n\nBut um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, moving around. No, I don't really feel restless. I just feel kind of stuck, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um, speaking slower? I... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really noticed my voice changing or anything. I mean, my throat has been kind of dry lately, but I think that's just the heating system in the clinic. 

They crank the heat up so high, it feels like a sauna in my office. I bought this little desk humidifier to try and help, it's shaped like a cactus. It lights up in different colors, which is kind of cool, but it goes through water so fast I'm constantly refilling it in the bathroom. The bathroom sink has this weird faucet where you have to hold the handle down the whole time or it shuts off. It's impossible to wash both hands at once. Someone left a note taped to the mirror complaining about it, written in bright pink highlighter. It looked like the same handwriting as the person who leaves passive-aggressive notes on the fridge about people not throwing away their old Tupperware. There's this one container of what looks like spaghetti that's been in there since I started. It's completely fuzzy now. I don't know why nobody just throws the whole thing away. I remember when I lived in the dorms my freshman year, my roommate left a bowl of cereal under his bed for a month. It smelled so bad we had to keep the window open in January. He was a weird guy, he used to sleep with his socks on but only halfway pulled up. 

But, uh... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, speaking slower. Yeah, I don't think I'm talking any slower than normal. It's more just the thinking part that's hard.
